Linux FADV_DONTNEED is advisory only; require original/Candidate matched 100k physical SHA, 2000 correct Range/SHA cold/warm, observed read_bytes, CPU/RSS and p95/p99 before classifying suitability. Never conflate Linux hot-cache Pack speed with online Blob migration, WAN, iOS/Android physical PWA or client rendering. Production AFTER=N/A unless separate evidence-gated product work; never touch Race or durable task cancellation.

- **CAS-A05 100k distinct small-CAS physical Pack/GC experiment:** consult [CAS-A05 small Blob Pack performance](docs/cas-small-pack-performance.md), [central tested-workload registry](docs/performance-test-registry.md), and [six-platform coverage](docs/performance-platform-coverage.md) before retesting. Actual 100k SHA-distinct on-disk Local.Put/Open/Delete and 95k physical GC must be measured on one runner before comparing any test-only pack/index/atomic-generation candidate; preserve all full SHA/Range/survivor/GC and original CPU/RSS/st_blocks/write_bytes evidence. Offline Pack is not production ContentBlob or Owner/Version/remote storage migration; no Race or durable task ownership changes.

- **LP-P002 Desktop Agent loopback 100k physical request layer:** consult [Desktop Agent 100k Native IPC Performance](docs/localpush-desktop-agent-ipc-100k-performance.md) after [LP-P001 Native Go baseline](docs/localpush-native-physical-performance.md). Distinguish actual authenticated loopback HTTP plus test-only scanner adapter from real owning-device Controller↔Server credentials, Electron Renderer/Main, durable Push/Pull, and actual installed PWA. Freeze native 100k physical shape, CPU/RSS/write bytes/authorization/no-private-path, take source-exact CURRENT n3 first, and accept unchanged production when below budget. Do not treat absent candidates as cloud deletes; preserve durable task ownership.

- **LP-P001 100k native physical inventory performance:** before repeating any real Device Backup local-root scan, read [LocalPush 100k Physical Performance](docs/localpush-native-physical-performance.md), the [deduplicated performance registry](docs/performance-test-registry.md) and [cross-platform matrix](docs/performance-platform-coverage.md). Validate 100k actual distinct native inode/file bytes and Root identity with full CURRENT/PREVIOUS journal and advisory delta, not just 100k SourceItem metadata; report Go/Linux-only separately from actual Desktop Agent IPC/real Push and six client platforms. The preexisting production baseline MUST be measured before making a performance optimization, and an advisory absent candidate is never evidence of remote deletion or a reason to cancel durable user tasks. All new valid/invalid original n3 and source SHA evidence belongs in the same one-work-commit PR and permanent raw archive; no Race changes.

- **CAS-A03-d2 fixed-block offline benchmark (not production CDC):** continue the existing [CAS-A03 benchmark](docs/cas-version-performance.md) only with a new workload ID, matched 4GiB high-entropy full-file production Local.Put versus *isolated* fixed 8MiB chunk PoC using the same A/B bytes on one runner, alternate A/B order, n>=3, real st_blocks, /proc/self/io, complete SHA + Range, physical restore, reference sweep and persistent raw evidence. An offline candidate never proves online FileVersion/owner/transfer/production GC or six client platforms; do not change product for structural preference.
- **CAS-A03 4GiB local edit version cost:** for whole-file versus 1MiB interior edit, first read [CAS-A03 Storage Version Benchmark](docs/cas-version-performance.md). This is a server-local physical SHA/CAS test distinct from first-upload 4GiB transfer throughput; do not infer CDC implementation, FileVersion database integrity or six-platform acceptance from fixed-block theoretical sharing. Record valid original n≥3 bytes/CPU/RSS/full-restore evidence before any production chunking change.

- **Server multi-generation backup evidence:** before any backup format, tar or chunking experiment, read [docs/backup-performance.md](docs/backup-performance.md). Distinguish a real physical file-data tar-stage n3 from a full PostgreSQL-consistent Server backup or six-platform end-to-end performance; preserve the current restore contract pending proven same-source A/B and complete recovery safety.

- **Blob/CAS architecture and deduplication audit:** before proposing changes to content hashing, refcounts, upload assembly, source ingest, thumbnail cache, large-file versioning, GC, or backup storage format, read [Blob/CAS Architecture Audit](docs/blob-cas-dedup-architecture-audit.md) alongside the [performance registry](docs/performance-test-registry.md) and [six-platform coverage matrix](docs/performance-platform-coverage.md). Preserve the immutable SHA-256 CAS identity, owner isolation, reference integrity, resumable transfer and restore safety. Treat the audit's unmeasured ideas (CDC, small-object packs, zero-copy, incremental backups) as hypotheses until frozen original n>=3 resource/byte/latency/integrity evidence establishes a need; never replace an accepted production hot path for architectural preference alone.

- **Mandatory tested-workload registry and deduplication:** before starting or continuing **any** performance track, read [`docs/performance-test-registry.md`](docs/performance-test-registry.md) FIRST, and follow its archived raw evidence and the matching canonical subsystem document. Search by the complete workload identity (surface/request path, logical count versus distinct physical CAS bytes/codecs, platform and transport, viewport/cache/scroll/cancel lifecycle, environment and sample method). Do **not** repeat an equivalent accepted exploratory 10k/100k/large-transfer test or retest a previously rejected unchanged candidate simply because a new chat or branch started. New production hot-path changes, measured regressions, genuinely untested workload dimensions, approved new budgets, and mandatory final exact-evidence-head CI revalidation are legitimate repeat reasons; record why. An evidence directory alone is not a passing benchmark.
- **Mandatory registry update in the same one-work-commit PR:** every new measured/failed/rejected/invalid performance workload, including a new A/B candidate or viewport-to-Go cancellation test, must update the central registry **and** its subsystem canonical document with a stable workload ID, physical-vs-metadata dataset shape, layer/platform, BEFORE/AFTER or N/A with units, sample count and frozen budgets, resource/byte/request correctness, exact source+CI+raw-evidence links, acceptance/rejection/blocked state and the specific next unmeasured gap. Retain old unsuccessful or invalid evidence; do not erase history or infer timing gains from cache state, runner differences, failed operations, or synthetic/mock-to-real comparisons.

- Before starting or continuing a performance task, inspect only the materially related open branches/PRs/MRs and the canonical performance document for that subsystem. Continue a viable related branch before opening a duplicate implementation. Already-merged, superseded, or abandoned performance branches must not be treated as active work.
- Every performance track must have a canonical document under `docs/` (for FileExplorer, use `docs/file-explorer-performance.md`). The document is part of the performance contract, not optional release notes.
- Every performance PR/MR must update its canonical performance document in the **same change** with the current status and evidence for that specific work item. Record at minimum: status, workload/data shape, scale, benchmark or trace command/tool, sample count when applicable, BEFORE baseline, AFTER/current result, units, delta, acceptance/rejection decision, regression budget when one exists, and the next action.
- Performance optimization is measurement-gated: **measure the current implementation first on the stable named workload before changing production code**. If the current result is already within the documented budget/acceptance target, keep the implementation unchanged and record that decision. If it is not, state the acceptance threshold before experimenting, make the smallest performance change, then rerun the **same workload, environment, units, and sampling method**. Keep the production change only when the improvement is material and repeatable beyond normal benchmark noise and no important CPU/RSS/I/O/request-count/correctness dimension regresses; otherwise revert the production change and record the experiment as **Rejected / no material gain** with its BEFORE/AFTER evidence.
- Use explicit status labels such as **Planned**, **Benchmarking**, **Measured baseline**, **In progress**, **Accepted**, **Rejected**, and **Merged**. Do not leave a completed optimization documented as merely planned, and do not leave an active benchmark without a status.
- If a change has no comparable wall-clock BEFORE/AFTER measurement, mark it clearly as **unmeasured / complexity-only** and do not quote a timing or percentage speedup. Structural improvements such as bounded concurrency, fewer allocations, fewer SQL round-trips, or bounded retained metadata may be documented with those exact structural metrics.
- Benchmark-only PRs/MRs must write their measured output back into the canonical document before merge. CI logs are transient evidence, not the durable performance record. If the benchmark result is produced by CI, amend the documentation with that exact result and rerun the authoritative validation because the source tree changed.
- Keep accepted and rejected experiments in the performance document. A rejected index, cache, batching policy, or concurrency change should retain its measured data and rejection reason so the same low-value experiment is not repeated later.
- Large-scale scenarios must use stable, named workloads. When a subsystem claims 10k/100k scalability, document the exact logical item count, viewport/range behavior, thumbnail/cache state where relevant, and whether the run measures pure controller CPU, browser/Electron rendering, network/IPC transport, decode/generation, or end-to-end latency.
- Performance CI jobs should be scoped to the branch/workload that needs them unless they are stable enough to become a permanent regression gate. Do not turn a noisy exploratory benchmark into a required global CI gate before its variance and budget are understood.
- **Viewport/request-scoped cancellation performance:** when a virtual range, thumbnail or preview is no longer consumed after scrolling, navigation, view/window teardown, or lifecycle changes, propagate cancellation through the owning Web `AbortSignal` or Desktop Renderer → Main → Agent IPC down to the HTTP request context and Go handler; measure actual upstream abort, active request count and payload waste, not only stale-result suppression. Scoped cancellation must not terminate durable Task Center uploads, downloads, synchronization, deletion, or shared background maintenance merely because a window changes. Reused/background tasks need their own lifecycle and consumer-aware semantics.

- **Mandatory cross-platform performance acceptance (2026-10-12):** treat **native Desktop Electron**, **wide Web browser**, **Mobile Web browser**, **desktop installed PWA**, **Android installed PWA**, and **iOS installed PWA** as distinct workload dimensions; read the [cross-platform matrix](docs/performance-platform-coverage.md) together with the [deduplicated workload registry](docs/performance-test-registry.md) before assigning a new performance ID. A green desktop 100k test does not qualify a mobile browser/PWA; browser-sized mobile emulation is not a phone; opening Chrome with `--app` or forcing `display-mode: standalone` does not prove OS installation. For Gallery and FileExplorer, cover cold/warm 100k physical JPEG/H264/Live still/Live motion/RAW/HEVC/LIVP as applicable, real shared Grid/Details/Timeline virtual-scroll windows, decoded pixels/posters, bounded DOM/CPU/RSS/network and cancellation all the way to actual Go `Request.Context().Done`; distinct source codecs and real bytes must be reported. For 1/4 GiB transfers and 100k synchronize/delete, test real CAS integrity, progress, task-owned cancellation, pause/restart/resume and owner permissions by platform, including lossy/slow networks; keep view/window unload distinct from durable Server task ownership. Keep every untested device/mode **Not measured**, and record separate exact-source n≥3 CPU/RSS/latency/bytes evidence per platform (and actual installed-device observations when available); never infer iOS background execution from Web/SW/desktop CI or pretend physical iOS/Android measurements were performed. All completed new tests must update the existing central registry, platform matrix, relevant subsystem document and stable raw evidence together in the same one-work-commit PR; no repeated accepted workloads merely to fill a grid.

## Sidebar account quota versus storage-host capacity (2026-10-12)

- The persistent Web/Desktop Sidebar footer must be explicitly labeled **我的云端存储** and must remain **current-account** even while an administrator visits global pages. `physical_used_bytes` from `/me/quota` is owner-scoped deduplicated `storage_key` accounting, **not** independently attributable physical bytes on the host filesystem. For positive `quota_bytes`, only current-account used/quota/percent belongs to the primary progress bar.
- For unlimited account quota (`quota_bytes=0`), retain the user-requested server storage disk available space and optionally an explicitly named **服务器存储磁盘（非个人配额）** percent when both trustworthy disk total/available are provided. Never subtract account charges from host filesystem occupancy to show fake personal-vs-other physical segments. Unknown disk denominator must not fabricate a percentage; full disk warning is not an account quota error. Preserve both apps' shared footer and 52px App Frame. See [`docs/sidebar-capacity-scope.md`](docs/sidebar-capacity-scope.md).

## Local-storage cache scope contract (2026-10-12)

- The user/device area “本地存储” is **client-scoped, not the logged-in account's cloud storage**. Web/Mobile Web browser `Cache Storage` cleanup affects every cache namespace for the current browser profile and xDrive Origin, potentially shared by accounts used there; `navigator.storage.estimate` is an origin aggregate, not a per-account or guaranteed reclaimable-byte metric. Desktop Electron HTTP `session.defaultSession` cache likewise is not per-account, and is separate from current-device Agent on-demand file cache and offline pinning.
- Every shared Local Storage page and destructive cache confirmation must disclose the current browser/client/Agent scope and any cross-account regeneration impact. Never claim the operation clears a Server account quota, all other browser origins/profiles, or globally stored CAS files. Existing Web Cache Storage, Electron `clearCache` and Agent `releaseCache`/CfAPI contracts stay untouched. See [`docs/local-storage-scope.md`](docs/local-storage-scope.md).

## Instance-wide system maintenance entry (2026-10-12)

- The only UI entry for host-level **服务端更新** is administrator **管理 → 系统维护** (Web app `admin-maintenance`, Desktop matching view); never embed it in personal Settings or the 11-service **系统依赖** editor. Reuse `XDriveSystemMaintenancePage` with the existing shared update controller/card/confirmation and the existing Web REST vs Desktop Agent IPC adapters. The Server `requireAdmin` and restricted host command boundary remain authoritative; the Desktop *client* updater stays device-local in Settings.
- Host updates are durable and cannot be canceled merely by a window close or App navigation. Preserve 52px App Frame, Web hash/deep-link and compact navigation, Desktop role-demotion guard, capability-unavailable feedback and unchanged Push/Pull/Task Center. See [`docs/system-maintenance.md`](docs/system-maintenance.md).

## Administrator Web/Desktop functional parity (2026-10-12)

- **Explicit product decision:** Desktop administrator navigation and functionality must equal Web, not a permitted platform exception. In the identical order: 用户管理, 全局后台任务, 审计日志, 全局存储, 系统依赖, 系统维护. No read-only placeholder for Web write operations.
- Cross-platform administrator views must be one shared MUI component per page, fed by Web's authenticated REST or Desktop's explicit authenticated Renderer → Main → Agent IPC → Go Client → Server admin API. Do not export tokens into Desktop Renderer, use a generic privileged URL/path proxy, or weaken Server `requireAdmin()`, self/last-admin invariants or audit. Old Agents show an unavailable explanation instead of a nonfunctional button.
- Complete and merge C1 user management, C2 virtualized audit, C3 full global storage, and **C4 personal/global background task UI parity**, including tests, before reporting finished Sidebar parity or resuming the performance-only mainline. Track progress and evidence in [`docs/sidebar-admin-parity.md`](docs/sidebar-admin-parity.md).

## Sidebar privilege partitions (2026-10-12)

- **Before “管理” = signed-in owner/device operations only; after “管理” = administrator-only instance/global operations.** The shared Web/Desktop MUI Sidebar and compact Mobile Web App switcher must preserve the same section ownership and relative order. User-owned files, Gallery, Push/Pull, personal tasks and cloud storage stay before the boundary; device-local caches, conflicts and diagnostics also stay before it and must disclose their local scope. “全局后台任务”, user management, audit, instance-wide storage and system dependencies belong only to the role-gated “管理” section, never to the core destination list or a bottom extension after management. This is **navigation presentation**, not an authorization substitute: protect global endpoints with Server admin checks and preserve owning-device authorization separately.
- Preserve existing workspace route keys, 52px App Frame header, task navigation/deep links, user-only tasks and Desktop device-only diagnostics; do not collapse global maintenance into a personal task or infer that a Desktop device operation is instance-global. Tests must verify Web/Desktop full Sidebar and compact Mobile menus for ordinary/admin role and the admin-boundary ordering.

- The administrator destination is displayed as **全局后台任务**, explicitly meaning Server-side tasks across users and system maintenance; ordinary **任务** remains current-owner/local-scoped. Reuse the stable `global-tasks` workspace key, `tasks?scope=global` deep links, `requireAdmin` and Server `control_actions`. Do not conflate the list with transient Web/Desktop network transfer popovers.
- **C4 Task Center UI parity:** personal and global Server background tasks default to one shared MUI task card (status, progress, elapsed time, actions); global additionally shows actual owner/system/instance context and can optionally use a dense table on wide viewports, but mobile always uses cards. Personal FileOperation undo/redo/conflict actions and local transfers do not belong to global Server tasks. Do not change task ownership, `control_actions`, pagination or durable-task cancellation when unifying UI. See [`docs/transfers-and-tasks.md`](docs/transfers-and-tasks.md).

## UI policy

- **Gallery Mobile Web / wide Web feature-parity is mandatory (2026-10-10).** Follow `docs/mobile-gallery-ios27.md`. iOS 27 Photos is the high-fidelity mobile-only **presentation and interaction** reference; Mobile Web MUST expose every Gallery operation that wide Web exposes (directly, by Collections, More, selection toolbar, Search or inspector), preserving permissions, selection scope, errors, task receipts, album semantics, media/Live/RAW/Viewer operations and return identity. Both layouts use ONE Web `XDriveMediaGalleryPage`, ONE Web REST `MediaGalleryDataSource`, shared `VirtualCollection`/VirtualGrid/VirtualTimeline, common selection/query/edit/Properties/Viewer and Server Range contracts. No separate mobile Gallery endpoint, media index, dense item collection, business controller or alternative Viewer. Responsive preference defaults may differ only as presentation settings; Server search/filter/sort capabilities and logical results must be equivalent. Verify mobile/wide crossover at 899/900px, 10k/100k range behavior and end-to-end task results; never count static source checks as physical iOS acceptance. Keep the full-viewport Mobile App Frame and 52px header unchanged.

- **Gallery Places map provider (2026-10-10, normative):** Web/Desktop/Mobile Web render maps **only through Baidu Maps Server API** via the authenticated xDrive Server; AK never enters renderer code or browser URLs. The original offline SVG world/graticule map and any alternate/self-hosted/offline map fallback are **not supported**. When the provider is disabled, AK is missing, HTTP fails, or an old Agent/Server is in use, keep the map region visible with a clear unavailable/configuration message, not another map. GPS facets/location list and optional GeoNames place-label metadata are separate from map rendering and remain available. See `docs/baidu-map-server-api.md`; update tests if this contract changes.
- **Baidu Server AK admin configuration (2026-10-10, normative):** The administrator **系统依赖** page may edit, disable, clear and **explicitly reveal** only the Baidu Server AK. Reuse the Yike Cookie temporary reveal UI: masked by default, deliberate click, audited server-side reveal, a no-store privileged response, and automatic hiding after 30 seconds or on view/account change. Normal status/config APIs never include plaintext. Encrypt and persist the AK using xDrive's versioned server-side credential keyring; no plaintext database row, logs, audit metadata or status JSON. Enforce Server-side admin authorization, revision conflicts, and explicit clear confirmation. New map requests must use the latest persisted effective configuration **without restarting Server, Docker, uploads, downloads or sync**. Only missing bootstrap encryption keys may require a one-time deployment restart. Saved disabled/cleared configuration must not fall back to an old environment AK; unavailable Baidu must never fall back to another map.

- **Mobile follow-up scope is normative.** Follow the user-approved order in `docs/mobile-web-followups.md`; do not turn unselected iOS comparison suggestions into implementation work. Below 900 CSS px, FileExplorer exposes only its current browsing context with **no tab bar or hidden internal tab commands**, including with attached mouse/keyboard. Preserve the same mounted workspace/history and desktop tab state for wide return. Record actual 44px location/filter hit targets, focus/lifecycle behavior and renderer evidence separately from the physical iOS/Android browser/installed-mode, keyboard, safe-area and VoiceOver matrix.

- **Mobile Web App Frame and full-screen layout are normative (2026-10-09).** Read `docs/mobile-web.md` and `docs/web-app-runtime.md` before changing Web App navigation, FileExplorer, Gallery, or Viewer. Below 900 CSS px, the **App Frame**, not the inner scrolling `main`, fills the entire dynamic viewport (x=0, y=0, full width/height). Its own 52px title bar (plus genuine top safe area) owns an explicit left **exit current App** action and top-right app-switch and upload/download actions; inner `main` receives the remaining height and retains its mounted scroll owner. No global floating navigation trigger, permanent global AppBar/footer/sidebar, reserved global spacer or desktop outer padding may cover App content/status. App switching is a Drawer reached from the App title bar; account/settings stay reachable inside it. Workspace App exit restores the previous App history entry, NOT the FileExplorer parent directory; a direct deep-link without a caller falls back to Overview. Viewer uses its existing full-viewport overlay header and own return semantics; never remount its caller. Mobile short tap opens, stationary hold shows context menu, and held move drags only when a valid destination/operation exists. Explicit Select and keyboard/menu alternatives remain; mobile file/photo tiles must not overlay Favorite/Info/More action buttons, but compact noninteractive Live/video/sync/duplicate badges are allowed. Preserve native scroll/pinch, Live Photo hold-to-play, text selection, Desktop controls, and browser Back/Forward. Validate actual Frame/header/main/status geometry, safe areas, long hold vs scroll/drag cancellation, 899/900px, 10k/100k virtualization, and iOS/Android device results separately from simulation.
- **Web/Mobile Web Files functional equivalence (2026-10-10):** Every wide-Web FileExplorer business capability is required on Mobile Web: operation permissions and validation, create/upload/download/copy/cut/paste/rename/move/delete, undo/redo, clipboard and path-copy, Search/filters/sort/grouping, folder Properties/statistics/provenance, thumbnails/media, favorites/recent/quick access, tags/saved searches, trash, share, history/navigation, errors/retries and cancellation. **Only internal multi-tab UI and its commands** may be omitted on Mobile due to screen size; replace any other dense desktop affordance with a reachable touch/keyboard/menu alternative. Both layouts must use the same Web REST adapters and stateful business controllers, **shared virtual collection** with Server-authoritative paged ranges, grouping and sparse-window cancellation, and shared `ui/shared` MUI business/thumbnail/Properties components. Mobile may independently render iOS Files chrome and layouts but must not fork transport, authorization, list pagination semantics, or business actions. Record a paired wide/375px/390px/899px/900px functional/permission/error regression matrix, 10k/100k bounded-list evidence and physical iOS/Android results separately. Treat any unverified or missing capability as a gap, not as parity.
- **iOS 27 Files 1:1 reference contract (2026-10-10):** `docs/mobile-files-ios27.md` is the explicit and version-locked visual/interaction acceptance baseline for Mobile Web Files, **not merely an iOS-inspired style guide**. Match Apple's iOS 27 Files behavior and layouts for corresponding real features, with screenshot-diff and interaction-state evidence before claiming 1:1. Preserve xDrive's full-dynamic-viewport App Frame and its separate **52px global App Header**, upload/download action and App switching, Web/Desktop shared Server/API and one shared workspace/virtual-collection/windowing/thumbnail/Properties contract. Only internal multi-file tabs may be omitted from Mobile Web; all other available wide-Web file functions require reachable mobile controls. Never fabricate a Shared-to-me view, folder-scoped Search, system document scanner, folder colors or iOS byte-perfect assets without the underlying functionality or measured licensed reference. Where Apple's current tab model or native capabilities differ from existing xDrive functionality, record the precise open gap rather than falsely claiming native parity. The new iOS 27 benchmark supersedes vague "iOS style" descriptions, but does not remove the user-approved functionality, security or existing App Frame.
- **Mobile Web Files iOS presentation (Q1–Q6, 2026-10-09):** Read `docs/mobile-files-ios.md` before modifying Files. Below 900 CSS px Web Files renders a dedicated Mobile Files component, not the reduced Desktop `XDriveFileExplorer` surface. Keep the internal Files-only Recent/Browse/Favorites rail, per-account Browse-home/last-folder+scroll contract, collapsible all-files Search, top-right More command menu and blue-folder/two-line List/Grid. Reuse existing Web REST/workspace/controllers and Viewer; do not add Mobile-only API, fake folder-scoped Search, global floating/bottom navigation or per-item overlay buttons. Native iOS/Android tests remain distinct from Chromium. Q1–Q4 App Frame rules above remain unchanged.
- Use **MUI (Material UI)** for all newly implemented Web and Desktop renderer UI. Prefer MUI layout, form, feedback, dialog, navigation, and display components over introducing new Ant Design or bespoke HTML/CSS component patterns.
- Existing Ant Design and bespoke Desktop UI does **not** need a one-shot migration. When a task materially touches an existing UI surface, opportunistically migrate the touched controls/components to MUI when doing so is low-risk and keeps the PR focused.
- `ui/shared` has two explicit layers: framework-neutral shared models/view-models/formatting/state derivation under `ui/shared/src`, and the canonical shared React/MUI component layer under `ui/shared/src/mui`.
- When Web and Desktop present the same business concept, first reuse or extend `@xdrive/ui/mui` instead of creating parallel app-local MUI implementations. Shared dialogs, forms, page/workspace composition, status/metric presentation, navigation chrome, Source UI, FileExplorer UI, Gallery UI, Task Center UI, and similar cross-client surfaces belong in the shared MUI layer when their interaction contract is materially the same.
- Keep platform adapters local. Web REST calls, Desktop Agent IPC, Electron/native-window chrome, OS integration, Desktop-only sync/cache/client-update behavior should stay in their owning app and feed shared components through typed props/adapters rather than being moved into shared rendering code.
- Do not duplicate cross-surface Source/status/formatting rules or common business presentation in Web and Desktop. Put framework-neutral semantics in `ui/shared/src`, common React/MUI rendering in `ui/shared/src/mui`, and only platform-specific orchestration in each app.
- When waiting on GitHub/GitLab CI for the current PR, use that time to **plan** the next small feature: inspect the relevant interfaces, tests, dependency chain, and branch strategy. Do not modify or open the next feature branch until the current small feature is merged, unless the work is an explicitly approved stacked dependency.

## Gallery documentation and product-contract policy

- G06 Node location/provenance must use owner-scoped live Node ancestry and
  SourceItem->Source relations separately. A synchronization-folder target
  ancestor is a current container, not proof of source-item origin.
  Do not infer remote origin from file name, capture time, or a Source folder
  label. Fetch full paths only on request; do not add N ancestor queries to
  a 100k Gallery range. Every new API endpoint must be included in the
  registered-route coverage manifest.


- G05 index coverage is explicitly on-demand and owner-scoped to **known
  non-trashed logical PhotoAssets**, reporting missing/failed/unsupported
  metadata. Never claim this equals whole-file-scan completion. The first
  sparse media range must not wait on index coverage, and Desktop must
  reject missing Agent capability instead of silently claiming success.
- G05 distinguish an editable filter/search draft from the last successfully
  applied Server query. Active chips and counts must describe the applied query;
  query changes invalidate the sparse range generation. The first image must
  not await camera/format facets or an all-media index-status scan. Label
  index coverage unknown until a separate, owner-scoped index readiness check
  actually succeeds. Keep a per-account bounded recent-search history and
  do not mix it across signed-in users or treat it as indexed suggestions.

- G04 Gallery tile presentation may toggle square crop versus uncropped
  media within the same bounded, square virtual cell. Do not describe
  the latter as variable-height masonry or change VirtualGrid/Timeline
  row geometry without the 100k media benchmark, original-item anchor
  checks, and explicit layout correctness tests. CSS mode changes must
  not trigger new original-byte reads or duplicate media-player surfaces.
- G04 manual-album cover updates require owner-scoped active collection
  and actual media membership verification; optimistic If-Match revision,
  transparent automatic-cover fallback when source media goes missing or is
  removed, and shared Web/Desktop transport. Never treat a selected cover as
  copied media bytes or allow cross-user images as covers.
- G04 album folder browser must use the shared MUI Web/Desktop organizer,
  consume only the Server's owner-scoped folder list and album_folder_id,
  preserve direct-child vs global-search scope, and explicitly handle
  revision conflicts, incomplete operations and nonempty-folder deletion.
  Never simulate folders via local pinned preferences or create duplicate
  PhotoAssets/PhotoCollectionAssets. Keep real mobile acceptance separate
  from Chromium fixtures.
- G04 persistent album folders must be owner-scoped Server metadata separate
  from synchronization-folder/file paths. Each folder write and album move
  must be revision-fenced, reject cross-user parents and ancestry cycles,
  and refuse deletion of nonempty folders. Only manual/smart albums can be
  reclassified; folder operations may never copy or delete logical media
  membership, physical bytes or provider-defined imported albums. Existing
  local pin/order state is independent of durable folder organization.
- G04 UI album pins, sorting and search operate on existing logical album
  records and must not create duplicate media membership. Account-scoped
  client preferences are local to each device; durable album cover and
  nested album-folder organization require authoritative, owner-scoped
  Server models and revision checks. Never claim local visual folders
  are synced albums or replace 100k virtualized media rendering with
  full media arrays.

- Chronology in Gallery/Viewer/Properties/Memories/date filters must use one
  validated IANA time-zone identity, not hardcoded UTC calendar labels, a
  browser-local-only filter or fixed +08:00 boundaries. Compute both local
  day boundaries independently across DST; preserve missing captured_at
  rather than substituting upload time. The Server owns sparse sort and range
  indexes; keep transient anchor IDs out of saved album and Viewer queries.
  Record separately when older Agents cannot support a new query capability.

- Before a Gallery, media Viewer, or media Properties change, read
  `docs/gallery-product-roadmap.md` and `docs/gallery-ios-kfs-audit.md`; also read
  `docs/preview-engine.md` for rendering/Properties and `docs/mobile-web.md` for touch
  or viewport changes. The audit is dated evidence, so verify the current shared code,
  Web/Desktop adapters, and materially related PR state before choosing new work.
- Keep **Verified baseline**, **In progress**, **Proposed**, and **Validation pending**
  distinct. Update affected canonical contracts and follow-up status in the same
  delivery, with the actual commit/PR and relevant validation evidence. Do not turn an
  implemented foundation into a new backlog item, or an unmerged/disabled/mock control
  into a completed feature.
- Preserve the accepted Gallery ordinary-click/tap-to-Viewer and common **属性**
  direction for active, permitted media, explicit/modifier selection, one shared media
  Properties content layer, and one active media player when opening Properties. Trash
  remains restricted to Properties and must not open Viewer or fetch original/Live
  motion resources. Check the implementation
  status rather than reverting to older double-click/Info-only wording. Gallery
  activation must not silently change FileExplorer's normal selection/open contract.
- KFS is a reference for useful interaction and information design, not an authority
  for xDrive semantics. Keep **同步文件夹** terminology, shared MUI/adapters,
  evidence-based media grouping/classification, local canonical metadata, remote
  read-only defaults, non-copying album memberships, and bounded VirtualCollection
  behavior. Do not copy filename/time-based Live Photo pairing or all-items DOM
  materialization.

## Cloud storage inventory policy

- **`docs/storage-inventory.md` is normative** for Server physical-storage accounting, cache classification, host-path display, and storage cleanup. Read and follow it before adding or changing any Server-side persistent cache, derivative, temporary directory, database/storage sidecar, backup class, or other xDrive-managed file class.
- Every administrator storage-inventory row must expose the resolved **host absolute path**. Never show environment templates such as `$XD_FILES_DATA_DIR/...`, unresolved relative paths, or container-only mount paths such as `/data/...` as though they were host locations.
- Any new persisted Server file class must define its inventory category, resolved host path, byte-accounting relationship, canonical/regenerable/temporary/read-only status, and safe cleanup semantics in the same change. Unknown/unclassified xDrive-reserved data is counted but is never automatically deleted.
- Cache cleanup may remove only explicitly regenerable or lifecycle-safe temporary data. It must never delete CAS/legacy canonical content, PostgreSQL data, backups, trash/history canonical content, Source identities/bindings/credentials, PhotoAsset/PhotoResource/PhotoMetadata canonical data, host configuration/program/log/state data, or unclassified files.
- Do not broaden API-container host access merely for storage accounting. Host-only directories are measured by the existing host-control boundary and exposed to the Server as aggregate counts/sizes plus already-resolved display paths; do not mount secret-bearing host directories into the API container.
- Web and Desktop must use the shared `XDriveCloudStoragePage` and shared storage models. Keep REST, Electron, and Agent IPC as thin platform adapters instead of forking storage presentation or cleanup semantics.
- Physical totals must be non-overlapping at their declared aggregation level. In particular, backup subcategories are detail rows under the backup root, and file-data cache/staging categories are detail rows under the file-data root; do not double-count child categories in `total_managed_bytes`.

## Asynchronous task and cancellation policy

- Keep cancellation work **scoped to the feature or subsystem currently being changed**. Do not start a repository-wide cancellation retrofit merely because another asynchronous subsystem is missing a control. Existing async cancellation gaps are handled when that subsystem is materially touched, explicitly requested, or becomes necessary for correctness.
- New or materially reworked long-running, user-visible asynchronous work should design safe cancellation as part of that work when cancellation is meaningful. Web and Desktop must use the same business semantics through platform adapters; do not intentionally ship one client with cancellation and the other without it for the same cross-client operation.
- **FileExplorer Folder Properties is the canonical request-scoped example.** Recursive folder statistics must run on Server data, never by walking the directory tree in Web/Desktop. The request is automatically cancelled when the Properties dialog closes, its target selection is replaced, or the owning surface unmounts. This operation does **not** create a durable Task Center entry and does not require a separate visible Cancel button.
- Web request-scoped cancellation uses `AbortController` / `AbortSignal`. Desktop must propagate the same cancellation through renderer -> Electron/Agent IPC -> Server request context rather than merely ignoring a late renderer result. Server handlers and database work must use the request context so cancellation can interrupt the recursive query.
- Cancelled request-scoped work is a normal lifecycle outcome: do not show a failure toast for an intentional abort, and never apply a stale result after a newer Properties request has started.
- Durable/background tasks that already participate in Task Center continue to use the existing shared task/control contract and Server-returned `control_actions`. Do not remove or fork existing cancellation semantics while working on an unrelated feature.
- When a task later touches an existing subsystem such as transfers, thumbnails, analysis previews, media indexing, Photo Intelligence, synchronization-folder runs, archive preparation, or maintenance, evaluate cancellation **inside that task's scope**. Add or improve cancellation when it is safe and useful, but do not proactively sweep those subsystems from an unrelated PR.
- Shared/singleflight work needs consumer-aware semantics **when that subsystem is being modified**: cancelling one waiter must detach that waiter, and must not incorrectly terminate work still needed by another waiter or a background owner.
- Cancellation must preserve storage and metadata integrity. Do not interrupt an indivisible commit that cannot be made safe. Temporary files, reservations, partial derived artifacts, and in-memory state must be cleaned up or left in a deterministic resumable/retryable state at safe boundaries.
- Tests for request-scoped cancellation must cover automatic abort on owner-surface close/replacement, stale-result suppression, no user-visible error on intentional abort, and actual propagation to the Server request context. Tests for durable task cancellation should follow that subsystem's existing lifecycle/control contract when it is touched.
- **FileExplorer non-performance design is normative.** Before changing FileExplorer behavior, selection, navigation, properties, drag/drop, shell integration, or other non-performance interaction contracts, read and follow `docs/file-explorer.md`. Performance-only work remains governed separately by `docs/file-explorer-performance.md`.

## Synchronization folder management policy

- User-facing naming follows the distinct application: **远程拉取** uses “拉取来源 / 来源名称 / 来源类型 / 来源设置” and never redundant “Pull” labels or “同步文件夹” in its list, create, detail, settings, confirmations, or feedback. **设备备份** uses device/backup wording. **同步文件夹** remains a technical/legacy Source and storage term only, not a generic label shared by both apps. The API/DB/legacy route IDs and physical target paths remain unchanged.
- Every Source settings surface must show the Server-resolved persisted xDrive target as a **read-only** location. A Pull-specific display may abbreviate the **known fixed Yike root only**, explicitly labelling the result a *storage subdirectory*; it must not change the stored `target_path`/Node ID or synthesize a new target from a template. All other targets show their actual resolved path.
- Ordinary settings saves must not silently move, rename, recreate, or rewrite the target directory. A target changes only through an explicit target-selection flow or a connector-owned identity/binding operation. Managed connectors such as Yike may validate or repair their deterministic hierarchy while binding a verified account, but changing schedule, run mode, status, ignore rules, or credentials for the same identity must not casually relocate data.
- Server-stored connector credentials remain encrypted at rest. Normal list/status/test APIs return only credential metadata and never plaintext. When a product requirement allows the signed-in owner to inspect a stored secret, expose a separate explicit **reveal** action that is owner-scoped, audited, `Cache-Control: no-store`, and returns only a connector-specific allowlisted secret field (for example Yike `cookie` or Synology Pull `password`) rather than the whole credential payload.
- Web/Desktop must never reveal a stored credential automatically. Show a mask by default, fetch plaintext only after an explicit user click, keep it only in renderer memory, automatically hide/clear it after about 30 seconds, and clear it when the settings surface closes. Never write revealed values to logs, diagnostics, local storage, clipboard automatically, or an editable replacement field.
- “Show stored credential” and “replace credential” are separate UI operations. Revealing the old secret must not populate or submit the replacement input. Leaving the replacement input empty preserves the current stored credential.
- New synchronization-folder connectors should adopt this target/credential behavior by default. If a connector does not store credentials on xDrive Server (for example a NAS-side Push agent with local credentials), omit server-side reveal rather than copying the secret into xDrive merely to satisfy UI symmetry.
- Web and Desktop should reuse the same shared MUI target/credential presentation primitives; connector/API state and mutation logic remain client-local.
- **Source/media design documents are normative.** Before changing a synchronization-folder connector, Source models, Source scheduling/reconciliation, or media projection triggered by Source imports, read and follow `docs/external-source-architecture.md` and `docs/photo-source-v2-roadmap.md`. If implementation and those documents disagree, reconcile the design explicitly in the same change rather than silently creating a second contract.
- **Preview Engine design is normative.** Before changing ordinary file preview, preview transport, FileExplorer Inspector preview, Gallery ordinary Image/Video presentation, or non-destructive media-edit transforms, read and follow `docs/preview-engine.md`. PDF/Video/Audio/Image must share the classifier, signed preview source, and `FilePreviewSurface`; non-destructive Gallery edits must remain versioned source-bound recipes rendered through that shared engine rather than app-local renderers or rewritten originals. Do not add media-specific playback APIs or a second binary preview stream. Keep Live Photo/EXIF/GPS/Favorite/Tags/People/Albums in the Gallery media-semantic layer.
- **Photo Intelligence design is normative.** Before changing automatic face detection/embeddings, person clustering, reverse-geocoded place names, visual/OCR intelligence, semantic embeddings/search, or their Gallery facets, read and follow `docs/photo-intelligence.md`. Photo Intelligence operates only on local `PhotoAsset` / `PhotoResource` / `PhotoMetadata` state; it must not import provider semantic metadata, mutate Source state, or overwrite user-managed Favorite/Tags/People/Description/albums.
- Durable Photo Intelligence person identity is **user intent**, not analyzer state. Persist long-lived people through `PhotoPerson / PhotoPersonAsset` and stable `person:v1:<uuid>` ids. Never use `PhotoFace.ID`, automatic cluster ids/keys, embedding similarity, or provider person ids as the durable user identity; never implicitly mirror durable-person mutations into legacy/manual `PhotoMetadata.PeopleJSON`.
- Synchronization-folder connectors are a **file synchronization layer only**. They may use provider APIs for authentication, file enumeration/identity/path/size/revision/digest hints, original-byte download, and proven change/tombstone contracts. Do not add provider-specific dependencies for canonical EXIF, GPS/geolocation, image/video technical parameters, thumbnails, Live Photo pairing, RAW/sidecar/burst grouping, people/tags/favorites/descriptions, or other media semantics.
- Canonical media metadata and relationships must be derived by xDrive from the preserved original files after `Node + File + CAS` commit. The same original must produce the same media result regardless of whether it came from Yike, Synology Photos, FileStation, Push, upload, Web, Desktop, FUSE, or CfAPI.
- Live Photo projection must be local and evidence-based: validated `.livp` structure, matching embedded Apple asset/content identifiers, or another deterministic local container/embedded identifier. Never make provider `live_type`, provider pair IDs, filename similarity, or timestamp proximity the canonical pairing source.
- Provider collection APIs may be retained only for file discovery or optional source provenance; collection/semantic API failure must never gate ordinary file backup, media indexing, or deletion inference.

## CI policy

- **Test-container images are runtime/toolchain images, not snapshots of the current business source tree.** For ordinary unit/integration/E2E/benchmark jobs that execute current repository code inside Docker, follow the normative contract in `docs/ci-test-images.md`: bake only stable OS/runtime/toolchain/system-library/third-party-model dependencies into the reusable test image, then bind-mount the checked-out business source, tests, scripts, and generated artifacts needed by that run. Source/test mounts should be read-only by default; writable mounts are limited to declared build/output/cache/runtime directories.
- Do **not** add a new test-image Docker stage that `COPY`s changing xDrive business source/tests and runs them during `docker build`. A normal source/test-only change must not require rebuilding a materially different test image. Rebuild the reusable test image only when its runtime dependency contract changes (base image, OS packages, language/runtime dependencies, native libraries, model/runtime assets, or equivalent image inputs).
- The exception is **exact-artifact/release-image validation**: when CI is explicitly proving the final server/Caddy/photo-face/client artifact that will be published, test the exact produced image/artifact byte-for-byte and do not mask or replace its packaged business code with a bind mount. When both fast source-mounted testing and final-image validation are needed, keep them as separate stages with separate purposes.
- Existing test-image paths that still bake mutable business code are migration debt. Convert them to the mounted-code contract when that test image is materially touched; all newly added test-container paths must comply immediately. GitHub/GitLab equivalents must preserve the same contract.

- Full CI runs for GitHub pull requests or GitLab merge requests targeting `master`, and also for direct pushes to `master` on both providers. Ordinary pushes to short-lived feature/fix branches do not run full CI.
- GitHub `workflow_dispatch` and a GitLab Web/Run pipeline are the equivalent manual full-CI entry points.
- A successful GitHub PR CI run is the authoritative pre-merge test gate. After a GitHub PR merges, do not wait for or monitor the resulting `master` push CI before continuing with the next task; that pipeline runs asynchronously as a post-merge safety net. GitLab uses local validation before its direct `master` push and the resulting `master` pipeline as the authoritative post-push gate. Keep GitHub branches single-commit before PR CI so no late squash is needed solely for history cleanup.
- Client packaging follows **Build Once / Publish Exact Artifact**, with **Test Exact Artifact** as the stable-release gate. `desktop-linux`, `desktop-windows`, and `build-client-core` build the reusable client components; `package-linux-client` and `package-windows-client` assemble the only user-facing installers independently of ordinary source/server/web tests, so installer production continues even if an unrelated test fails. Exact-artifact tests wait for and consume only their corresponding installer artifacts. Publishing must consume those artifacts byte-for-byte and must never rebuild Electron or the client installer. A `master` snapshot may publish those exact CI-produced installers before exact-artifact tests finish; a stable `v*` tag must not publish until those tests and the full gate succeed.
- There is exactly one user-facing installer per platform: `xdrive-linux-amd64.deb` on Linux and `xDriveSetup-amd64.exe` on Windows. Do not reintroduce `xdrive-desktop-linux-amd64.deb`, `xDriveDesktopSetup-amd64.exe`, or a second Linux client installer filename.
- Release jobs should not rerun test suites that are already part of full CI. Server images follow **Build Once / Publish Exact Image**, with exact-image/full-suite validation required before stable promotion: `server-image`, `web`, and `caddy-image` build versioned candidate images and persist exact image artifacts; `server-backup` imports the exact server image instead of rebuilding it; release publication imports those same image artifacts, pushes immutable registry tags, and promotes them to `edge` for `master` or `latest` for a stable tag. A `master` snapshot may promote the exact CI-produced images before the remaining full-suite validation completes; a stable `v*` tag must wait for `final-gate`. The publish phase may build deployment assets, but it must not rebuild client installers or server images.
- GitHub `.github/workflows/release.yml` and GitLab `infra/ci/gitlab-release.yml` implement the same split release contract. A `master` push builds publishable installers/images in parallel with full CI and publishes the rolling `snapshot`/`edge` channel as soon as those exact artifacts pass fast publication-integrity checks; ordinary and exact-artifact tests continue independently and do not block the snapshot. A stable `v*` tag still requires the complete `final-gate` before any release publication or `latest` promotion. GitHub invokes the reusable publish workflow from the same CI run so it downloads exact artifacts without rebuilding. GitLab uses DAG `needs`/`rules:needs` so `master` publication can run ahead of the verify stage while stable tags remain gated.
- Release-grade GitHub and GitLab builds must publish the same named file set, use the same release/desktop version semantics, generate `SHA256SUMS.txt`, and publish the same three exact CI-produced server images. Within one provider pipeline, published client installer bytes and server image IDs must come from artifacts produced by that same pipeline. For stable `v*` tags those exact artifacts must be validated by the full gate before publication; for `master`, the same validation may complete after the rolling snapshot has already been published. The two providers may still produce different binary hashes or image IDs because their build hosts, signing environments, timestamps, and Docker builders differ.
- GitLab keeps release-job artifacts for 14 days for pipeline access, but durable release files are uploaded to the GitLab Generic Package Registry and attached to the GitLab Release. Do not use expiring job-artifact URLs as the long-term release source.
- When adding a release file, update the producer copy/chmod steps, the GitLab `release-assets` artifact paths, each provider's publisher input list, and the checksum manifest together; keep the parity contract test explicit enough to fail when any handoff omits the file.
- The server installer supports both registries. GitHub release artifacts bake `ghcr.io/<owner>` as the default image registry; GitLab release artifacts bake `$CI_REGISTRY_IMAGE`. At runtime, `XD_IMAGE_REGISTRY` overrides either default. Do not implement silent cross-registry failover.
- Release baking globally replaces the installer template's `@...@` values. Runtime template detection must use structural placeholder recognition rather than comparing against raw placeholder literals, because those comparison literals would be replaced too. Keep a regression test that executes a fully baked GitLab installer without explicit source/channel flags.
- GitLab 17.x job tokens cannot move repository tags. The rolling GitLab `snapshot` Release must publish `XDRIVE_RELEASE_COMMIT=<full SHA>` in its description, and both Desktop and Server update resolution must treat that validated marker—not the immutable Release tag target—as the authoritative master snapshot commit.
- GitLab `master` snapshot publication runs only when the instance exposes a non-empty `CI_REGISTRY_IMAGE`. On an instance without Container Registry, skip the GitLab release-build/promote/release jobs while the full CI continues normally. Stable `v*` tags must still enter the publication path, wait for the complete gate, and fail clearly when the required registry is unavailable; never invent a registry URL or fall back to another provider.
- Stable `v*` Windows releases require `XD_WINDOWS_SIGN_PFX_B64` and `XD_WINDOWS_SIGN_PFX_PASSWORD` during the CI artifact build on both providers. Master snapshots may be unsigned when those secrets are unavailable; the publish phase must never add or change a client signature because that would change the tested artifact bytes.
- Stable formal releases are cut from `v*` tags. Do not introduce `release/*` as a publication trigger unless the project explicitly adopts a separate stabilization-branch workflow; `master` remains the rolling snapshot/edge channel.

## GitHub / GitLab CI parity

- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `.gitlab-ci.yml` are two front ends for the same CI contract. Any change to CI jobs, platform coverage, toolchain versions, test/build commands, Docker/deployment validation, trigger semantics, or merge gates must update both files in the same code change.
- Keep these jobs one-to-one across both systems: `single-commit`, `desktop-linux`, `desktop-windows`, `go-linux`, `go-windows`, and `web`.
- Keep trigger behavior equivalent: GitHub PR-to-`master` corresponds to GitLab MR-to-`master`; pushes to `master` run full CI on both providers but rolling snapshot publication must not wait for that full CI to finish; `v*` tags run full CI and must pass `final-gate` before stable publication on both providers; GitHub `workflow_dispatch` corresponds to a GitLab Web/Run pipeline; GitHub cancel-in-progress corresponds to GitLab interruptible auto-cancel.
- `internal/cicontract/ci_parity_test.go` is the executable parity guard. It parses both YAML files, requires the same job set and key commands, and is part of the normal Go test suites. Do not weaken or bypass it to make one CI provider diverge.
- GitLab jobs use the same literal runner tags as the established GitLab runner setup: `linux` for Linux jobs and `windows` for Windows jobs. Do not indirect these tags through CI/CD variables unless the runner topology itself changes.
- GitLab Linux jobs must run in explicit Docker images, following the same pattern as the established GitLab project. Centralize image names and pinned Linux tool versions in `infra/ci/images.yml`; do not fall back to tools installed on the Linux runner host.
- The `linux` runner must use the Docker executor (or an equivalent container executor that honors GitLab's `image:` keyword). It must be x86_64 and provide access to a Docker daemon for jobs that execute `docker build`, `docker run`, or `docker compose`. With Docker socket binding, mount `/var/run/docker.sock` into job containers; an explicitly configured DinD endpoint is also acceptable. The job image provides Go/Node/Docker CLI tooling; the runner host should not be treated as the toolchain.
- The `go-linux` job uses a resource group because deployment tests intentionally exercise real Docker resources. `single-commit` and `go-linux` use the Go CI image; `desktop-linux` and `web` use the Node CI image. Windows jobs intentionally have no Linux `image:` and continue on the `windows` runner.
- Database integration tests in GitLab `go-linux` must use the GitLab PostgreSQL service alias (`postgres:5432`). Do not start PostgreSQL through the bound Docker socket and publish it to `127.0.0.1`: the Docker daemon host and the Docker-executor job container have different loopback namespaces, so host-published localhost ports are not a valid service endpoint for the job container.
- Electron packaging commands must pass `--publish never` in normal CI/build scripts; publishing is handled by the repository release workflow, not implicitly by electron-builder. GitLab uses `ELECTRON_MIRROR` and `ELECTRON_BUILDER_BINARIES_MIRROR` from `infra/ci/images.yml` for release-asset downloads, and Linux Electron/builder binary caches live under `$CI_PROJECT_DIR/.cache`. These mirror variables may be overridden by GitLab project/group CI/CD variables when an internal mirror is available.
- GitLab CI defaults to mainland-China-friendly dependency endpoints: `GOPROXY` falls back from goproxy.cn to Aliyun, then proxy.golang.org and direct on any error; `GOSUMDB=sum.golang.google.cn` preserves Go checksum verification; Node binaries prefer Huawei Cloud; Docker CLI prefers Aliyun; npm uses npmmirror. Official sources remain fallbacks. `HTTPS_PROXY`/`HTTP_PROXY` are honored normally, and `XDRIVE_CI_GITHUB_RELEASE_PROXY` may prefix GitHub Release downloads when an organization-approved proxy is available.
- Large CI tool downloads must use `scripts/ci/download-with-fallback.sh` so interrupted downloads resume, retry, and fall back between mirrors. Partial tool downloads and Go/npm caches live under `$CI_PROJECT_DIR/.cache`; GitLab caches them with `when: always` so a failed pipeline can resume on the same runner instead of restarting from zero.
- Go 1.25 is the minimum supported toolchain baseline. Canonical module-tidiness validation must run under an actual Go 1.25 toolchain (GitHub Go jobs and GitLab `go-linux`). Self-hosted GitLab Windows runners may use newer Go versions, but they must not run `go mod tidy`: use the committed module graph with `go mod download`, `go mod verify`, and `-mod=readonly` for tests/builds so a newer Go release cannot silently upgrade dependencies or raise their minimum Go requirement.
- Windows CI and upgrade tests must resolve the Inno Setup uninstaller from the xDrive uninstall registry entry's `UninstallString`; never assume the filename is `unins000.exe`, because Inno Setup may allocate `unins001.exe` or later names on persistent runners.
- Windows CI, installer, upgrade, rollback, and legacy-cleanup scripts must only stop xDrive processes in the script's own Windows session. A service runner must never terminate an interactive user's Desktop or Agent merely because the executable name matches.
- The Electron main process writes lifecycle diagnostics to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\xDrive\desktop.log` on Windows, rotates one 2 MiB backup, and uses a running-session marker to report force termination on the next launch. Keep lifecycle logging free of credentials, request bodies, and server tokens.
- Windows service-runner smoke tests must launch Desktop with isolated `LOCALAPPDATA` and `--user-data-dir`, verify the in-process `window_shown` and `startup_complete` lifecycle checkpoints, and clean up only processes created by that test. Do not use `MainWindowHandle` as the readiness signal because a service runner cannot reliably observe Electron HWNDs.
- The GitLab Windows runner is a Windows Shell executor configured with Bash/Git Bash. GitLab `script:` entries for Windows jobs must therefore be Bash commands; do not place raw PowerShell syntax directly in `.gitlab-ci.yml`. Use Bash wrapper scripts and invoke `powershell.exe` explicitly only for Windows-native operations such as certificate creation, Authenticode validation, registry checks, and installer smoke tests. The runner must provide Go >= 1.25, Node.js 22, npm, Git Bash, Windows PowerShell, Chocolatey, Windows CfAPI support, certificate creation/signing support, and permission to install/use Inno Setup.
- GitLab Windows jobs keep Go's module cache under `$CI_PROJECT_DIR/.cache/go-mod`, but GitLab must cache only `$GOMODCACHE/cache/download` rather than the extracted module tree. The extracted tree is read-only and may not survive cache archive/restore byte-for-byte. `default.hooks.pre_get_sources_script` removes the previous worktree copy before Git clean/fetch, then `scripts/ci/prepare-go-mod-cache.sh` verifies the restored download cache and automatically clears/redownloads it if verification fails. Bump the Windows Go/release cache-key version whenever this cache format changes. Do not replace this with a broad `GIT_CLEAN_FLAGS` exclusion for `.cache`, because persistent Shell-runner worktrees must not bypass GitLab cache isolation.

## Master protection

- Never force-push `master` or rewrite its published history.
- Never use `git push --force`, `git push -f`, or `git push --force-with-lease` against `master`.
- Repository branch protection/rulesets for `master` must keep force pushes and branch deletion disabled.
- GitHub changes must arrive through a tested short-lived branch/PR; do not bypass required CI checks. GitLab changes follow the explicitly allowed locally validated direct-to-`master` workflow above.
- Force-pushing a work branch is allowed only when needed to amend/rebase while preserving the branch's single-work-commit invariant, and only after verifying the target is not `master`. Do not accumulate multiple commits and use a late squash as the normal workflow.

Do not merge known failing or untested changes into `master`.

When multiple related unmerged branches exist, prefer dependency order. Dependent follow-on work may continue directly from the upstream work branch without waiting for its PR/MR to merge. Unrelated branches and PRs/MRs are outside the current task's workflow and must not block or reorder the work unless they create a real conflict or alter a required dependency. A dependent branch must contain exactly one work commit above its immediate dependency throughout development. Before it is made merge-ready after the dependency lands, rebase/reconstruct that same single commit onto the latest `master`, keep only its own delta, and require its own PR/MR CI to pass. Avoid rebasing or amending an already-green PR solely to create another CI run.

Prefer small, focused branches that remain single-commit from their first commit through merge, minimizing conflicts, avoiding late-squash CI churn, and keeping `master` history reviewable.


## Baidu Static Map administrator live effect proof P1-A2 (2026-10-11)

- Baidu remains the only Gallery map provider. Encrypted Server AK config changes apply to subsequent requests, but **saved/enabled is not remotely verified** until explicit admin testing.
- An administrator-triggered, current-revision-only proof sends one fixed public-coordinate 128x128 Static Map v2 HTTPS request from Server, only after a committed metadata-only audit. Never allow a user-supplied URL, endpoint, coordinate, AK, method or redirect; no environment proxy, bounded read/decode, short timeout and per-instance rate limit. Never expose key/plaintext URL in response or audit; failing audit prevents an external call.
- Verify the actual 200 PNG dimensions and full decode; no success for malformed, denied, or unreachable responses. After network, recheck enabled/revision/AK, rejecting concurrent changes.
- Web/Desktop share an ephemeral, current-revision-only verification status; reset on any draft edit, save/rotate/disable, page/account/source refresh. No persistent or fleet-wide verified label. This change must not touch user Source/同步文件夹 records, Docker, core restart, uploads, downloads or sync.


## Admin service lifecycle proof separation P1-S1 (2026-10-12)

- Keep `configuration_state`, `installation_state`, `health_state`, and `activation_state` independent across all 11 global service rows in Go Server → Go Client → Agent IPC → shared Web/Desktop MUI. Do not infer installed from configured, healthy from an untested secret, or applied from a working container/HTTP ingress with no desired-revision binding.
- Only real trusted runtime/model/index/Host Manager evidence may be `installation_state=observed`; real checks and expired trusted heartbeats may be `health_state=failed`. `activation_state=verified` requires exact desired-revision, service-specific effect proof (currently isolated Media Worker). Baidu AK live-request reading is `local-effective`, explicitly not remote authorization; Caddy remains `deployment-only` pending host no-lockout transaction, rollback and exact revision proof. Other unverified states are `unknown`, never green.
- Preserve Caddy `https_configured` (including explicit false) and `local_tls_state` through the Go Client/Desktop Agent service snapshot. Old Servers/Agents that omit new axes must show `unknown` on both Web and Desktop. This read-only overview may not expose or change per-user Source/同步文件夹 data, Docker, cloud credentials, DNS, mount paths, running uploads/downloads/sync or durable jobs.
- **Gallery Places iOS 27 staged delivery (2026-10-13, approved):** Follow [docs/gallery-places-ios27-roadmap.md](docs/gallery-places-ios27-roadmap.md) in strict order A1→A2→A3→A4→B1→B2→B3→C1. iOS 27 Photos is a 1:1 content/interaction acceptance target, not simply an aesthetic inspiration. Keep full-screen 52px App Frame, one Web/Mobile Web Gallery REST/DataSource/VirtualCollection/Viewer and Desktop Agent transport. Finish each stage with exact-head CI/Final Gate and clean up only related branches/PRs. A3 **review** of Baidu JSAPI 4.0's AK-protecting server proxy is authorized, not carte-blanche to ship an unauthenticated proxy or a browser-exposed AK. JSAPI requires a **browser-type AK**, not assumed interchangeable with the existing Server Static Map AK; any dynamic renderer must pass separate legal/licence, encryption, CSP, authentication, endpoint allowlist, quota, leakage and native-platform gates. Baidu remains the sole map provider; do not introduce an offline or alternate map. A1 owner-scoped paged GPS count and real representative coordinate work must land first. Source tests alone are not real-device iOS 27 acceptance.
- **Gallery Places dual-AK latest approval (2026-10-13; supersedes previous Server-AK-only restriction):** Baidu remains the **sole map provider**. The existing encrypted **Server AK** may be used only for the authenticated Baidu Static Map v2 API, and a separately encrypted **Browser-type AK** may be stored for JSAPI 4.0 under admin revision-fenced configuration. No Browser/Server AK may enter renderer source, JS, browser/Electron URLs, upstream-response bodies, logs, preload, Service Worker caches or user-visible state. A3a [licence/proxy threat review](docs/gallery-places-jsapi4-proxy-review.md) must merge before allowing runtime proxy activation; A3b1 browser-key config PR #1607 may be reopened on latest user approval with **runtime_enabled=false**, separate from actual runtime enablement. JSAPI 4.0 cannot ship until verified lawful entitlement, real allowed upstream host/path/method/query resources, same-origin auth for scripts/tiles, no credential leakage, cross-replica quota, CSP, Electron IPC sandbox, owner-scoped bounded media data, cancellation, and iOS/Android/Desktop acceptance. Keep the existing Baidu Static Map visible with explicit unavailable messaging until then; no wildcard proxy, key-type substitution, Google/OSM/offline map, detached mobile Gallery controller or second virtualizer. Preserve full-viewport 52px App Frame and owner-scoped real GPS.

- After merged A3b2c1 #1633 (exact-head complete CI/Final Gate), keep [Places iOS 27 roadmap](docs/gallery-places-ios27-roadmap.md) and [JSAPI 4.0 proxy/security review](docs/gallery-places-jsapi4-proxy-review.md) authoritative. A clear-cookie header does not revoke existing tokens. Continue `runtime_enabled=false`, Baidu sole Provider, encrypted separate Server Static AK and Browser JSAPI AK, full-screen 52px App Frame and shared Web/Mobile Web/Desktop Gallery/Range/VirtualCollection/Viewer.
- CI: isolated PostgreSQL integration tests check owner separation, independently opened replica connection, idempotent initialization, atomic increment, monotonic cutoff, rollback, disabled/must-change-password denial, foreign key and nil/invalid input. Standalone Go access-time predicate test proves still-valid old JWT cannot re-mint after revocation. These tests are **not** real Baidu entitlement, an enabled proxy or physical iOS/Android/Desktop acceptance.
